# V-10 Huge graph shows text

**You should see:** a graph of more than 2,000 things, its text shown in place of a drawing, under a banner with an icon that says the limit and the numbers in words. Obsidian stays responsive while it is checked: the note scrolls and the other block below it draws. The text has no Export button: with no drawing there is nothing to export, and an export is held to the same limits. The graph is real: graph theory (its people, theorems, algorithms and famous graphs, vertex by vertex) and this project (its requirements, tests, waves, workstreams and code, file by file), linked where the two meet.

**Test diagram:**

```gql

(gt_five_colour:Theorem {name: 'Five colour theorem', year: 1890, statement: 'Five colours suffice for any planar graph.'}),
(gt_heawood_conjecture:Theorem {name: 'Heawood conjecture', year: 1890, statement: 'A bound on the colours needed for maps on a surface of genus g.'}),
(gt_petersen_theorem:Theorem {name: 'Petersen’s theorem', year: 1891, statement: 'Every bridgeless cubic graph has a perfect matching.'}),
(gt_menger_theorem:Theorem {name: 'Menger’s theorem', year: 1927, statement: 'The fewest vertices separating two vertices equals the most disjoint paths between them.'}),
(gt_kuratowski_theorem:Theorem {name: 'Kuratowski’s theorem', year: 1930, statement: 'A graph is planar if and only if it has no subdivision of K5 or K3,3.'}),
(gt_ramsey_theorem:Theorem {name: 'Ramsey’s theorem', year: 1930, statement: 'Any large enough graph has a big clique or a big independent set; R(3,3) = 6.'}),
(gt_konig_theorem:Theorem {name: 'Kőnig’s theorem', year: 1931, statement: 'In a bipartite graph, a maximum matching is as large as a minimum vertex cover.'}),
(gt_whitney_isomorphism:Theorem {name: 'Whitney isomorphism theorem', year: 1932, statement: 'Connected graphs with the same line graph are the same, except K3 and K1,3.'}),
(gt_hall_theorem:Theorem {name: 'Hall’s marriage theorem', year: 1935, statement: 'A bipartite graph matches one side fully if every set of it has at least as many neighbours.'}),
(gt_konig_book:Theorem {name: 'First book on graph theory', year: 1936, statement: 'Theorie der endlichen und unendlichen Graphen.'}),
(gt_polya_enumeration:Theorem {name: 'Pólya enumeration theorem', year: 1937, statement: 'Counts graphs and other objects up to symmetry.'}),
(gt_wagner_theorem:Theorem {name: 'Wagner’s theorem', year: 1937, statement: 'A graph is planar if and only if it has neither K5 nor K3,3 as a minor.'}),
(gt_brooks_theorem:Theorem {name: 'Brooks’ theorem', year: 1941, statement: 'A connected graph needs at most Δ colours, unless it is complete or an odd cycle.'}),
(gt_turan_theorem:Theorem {name: 'Turán’s theorem', year: 1941, statement: 'The most edges a graph can have with no clique of r + 1 vertices.'}),
(gt_hadwiger_conjecture:Theorem {name: 'Hadwiger conjecture', year: 1943, statement: 'A graph with no K(t+1) minor can be coloured with t colours. Open.'}),
(gt_tutte_counterexample:Theorem {name: 'Tutte’s counterexample', year: 1946, statement: 'A 3-connected cubic planar graph with no Hamiltonian cycle.'}),
(gt_tutte_theorem:Theorem {name: 'Tutte’s theorem', year: 1947, statement: 'When a graph has a perfect matching, in terms of its odd components.'}),
(gt_dirac_theorem:Theorem {name: 'Dirac’s theorem', year: 1952, statement: 'A graph on n ≥ 3 vertices, each of degree at least n/2, is Hamiltonian.'}),
(gt_max_flow_min_cut:Theorem {name: 'Max-flow min-cut theorem', year: 1956, statement: 'The largest flow from source to sink equals the smallest cut between them.'}),
(gt_erdos_renyi:Theorem {name: 'Erdős–Rényi model', year: 1959, statement: 'Random graphs where each edge is present with the same chance.'}),
(gt_erdos_girth:Theorem {name: 'High girth, high chromatic number', year: 1959, statement: 'There are graphs with no short cycles that still need many colours.'}),
(gt_ore_theorem:Theorem {name: 'Ore’s theorem', year: 1960, statement: 'If non-adjacent vertices have degrees adding to at least n, the graph is Hamiltonian.'}),
(gt_erdos_gallai:Theorem {name: 'Erdős–Gallai theorem', year: 1960, statement: 'Which sequences of numbers are the degrees of a simple graph.'}),
(gt_berge_conjecture:Theorem {name: 'Perfect graph conjectures', year: 1961, statement: 'Berge’s weak and strong conjectures on perfect graphs.'}),
(gt_vizing_theorem:Theorem {name: 'Vizing’s theorem', year: 1964, statement: 'A simple graph’s edges can be coloured with Δ or Δ + 1 colours.'}),
(gt_ringel_youngs:Theorem {name: 'Map colour theorem', year: 1968, statement: 'The Heawood conjecture holds for every surface but the Klein bottle.'}),

(gt_karp_21:Theorem {name: 'Karp’s 21 problems', year: 1972, statement: 'Twenty-one problems, many on graphs, shown NP-complete.'}),
(gt_weak_perfect:Theorem {name: 'Weak perfect graph theorem', year: 1972, statement: 'The complement of a perfect graph is perfect.'}),
```

The block that must still draw while the huge one is checked.

```gql
// graph: small · SOURCE
(a:Thing {name: 'A'}),
(b:Thing {name: 'B'}),
(a)-[:NEXT]->(b)
```

<!-- expect: clean -->

**Do this:**

1. Open this note in reading view.
2. Look for the banner over the text of the first block, and read the limit and the numbers in it.
3. While it is up, scroll the note and watch the second block draw. Check the text has no Export button.
4. On the phone, open this note once the vault has synced, and do the same.

**Checklist line:**

> **V-10** A huge graph (over 2,000 things: graph theory and this project, made by a script) shows its text with a banner — an icon, and the limit and the numbers in words — and Obsidian stays responsive: the note scrolls and other blocks draw while it is checked. On the desktop and on the phone (§9, layout_contained; the tests run the worker in a Node thread, not a web view). The text has no Export button: with no drawing there is nothing to export, and an export is held to the same limits.

**Back to the checklist:** [V-10 in the device checklist](https://github.com/rogerbucks/thoughtknot/blob/main/packages/obsidian/test/device-checklist.md), on GitHub. Look for V-10 in its list.
